# Research Group subset-b-000596

Grouped research for ceph-client Linux Devicetree binding YAML schemas covering wireless, Xilinx Ethernet, NPU, NVMe, NVMEM, OPP, and early PCI bindings. Each source file section is delimited for deterministic splitting into source-tree-aligned per-file research documents.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/wireless/brcm,bcm4329-fmac.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/wireless/brcm,bcm4329-fmac.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Broadcom BCM4329 family fullmac wireless SDIO/PCIE devices`, a wireless controller binding. The schema description says: The Broadcom Single chip MAC part for the BCM4329 family and later Cypress chips in the same family named CYW4373 and similar. These chips also have a Bluetooth portion described in a separate binding. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input rather than executable kernel code; its practical role is to make DTS/DTB hardware descriptions match the resources, topology, and compatible strings expected by the relevant kernel subsystem before probe.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The public API is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses `oneOf` with 2 branchs and exposes 30 tokens: `brcm,bcm43143-fmac`, `brcm,bcm4341b0-fmac`, `brcm,bcm4341b4-fmac`, `brcm,bcm4341b5-fmac`, `brcm,bcm4329-fmac`, `brcm,bcm4330-fmac`, `brcm,bcm4334-fmac`, `brcm,bcm43340-fmac`, `brcm,bcm4335-fmac`, `brcm,bcm43362-fmac`, `brcm,bcm4339-fmac`, `brcm,bcm43430a0-fmac`, `brcm,bcm43430a1-fmac`, `brcm,bcm43455-fmac`, `brcm,bcm43456-fmac`, `brcm,bcm4354-fmac`, `brcm,bcm4356-fmac`, `brcm,bcm4359-fmac`, `brcm,bcm4366-fmac`, `brcm,bcm43752-fmac`, `cypress,cyw4373-fmac`, `cypress,cyw43012-fmac`, `infineon,cyw43439-fmac`, `pci14e4,43dc`, and 6 more. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `interrupt-names`, `brcm,drive-strength`, `reset-gpios`, `brcm,ccode-map`, `brcm,ccode-map-trivial`, `brcm,cal-blob`, `brcm,board-type`, `apple,antenna-sku`, `clocks`, `clock-names`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`; required properties collected from nested schema branches include `compatible`, `reg`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`, `minimum=0`, `maximum=32`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` describes MMIO ranges, bus child addresses, port selectors, or cell offsets; `interrupts` routes status, error, RX/TX, MSI, or completion IRQs; `interrupt-names` fixes resource ordering visible to drivers; `reset-gpios` captures board GPIO sequencing; `clocks` integrates with common clock, reset, PM, DMA/IOMMU, or reserved-memory providers; `clock-names` fixes resource ordering visible to drivers. The highest-risk ABI details are compatible identity, bus address, interrupt or wake wiring, calibration and OTP/NVMEM references, firmware names, frequency limits, power limits, and vendor board-data properties.

## Control Flow
Runtime control flow is supplied by dt-schema and kernel subsystem code, not by this YAML file. `dt_binding_check` loads the meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields, validates `minItems`/`maxItems` and enum/const constraints, validates embedded examples, and applies the closure rule. During boot, the compiled DTB node is matched by `compatible`; then platform, PCI, SDIO, SPI, MDIO, NVMEM, OPP, PCIe, netdev, wireless, accelerator, storage, clock, reset, regulator, IRQ, GPIO, IOMMU, and power-domain helpers consume the phandles and resource arrays that this schema validates. Child nodes are discovered declaratively through fixed names, pattern properties, address cells, and referenced child schemas.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The schema owns no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, register and interrupt ordering, phandle and cell counts, child-node names, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in board DTS files and shipped DTBs. Runtime state belongs to the matching drivers and hardware after probe, including wireless device probe, firmware boot, regulatory/channel capability selection, calibration selection, IRQ/wake state, netdev/wiphy registration, and runtime PM.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Arend van Spriel <arend@broadcom.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/net/wireless/wireless-controller.yaml#`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/string`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/string-array`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/uint32`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/uint8-array`. Integration points include cfg80211/mac80211, fullmac or softmac Wi-Fi drivers, SDIO/PCIe/SPI/platform buses, firmware and calibration loading, regulators, clocks, GPIOs, IRQs, NVMEM cells, and board DTS nodes. Driver integration is through OF/ACPI-adjacent match tables and subsystem helper calls; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings, resource arrays, child nodes, and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/net/wireless/broadcom/brcm80211/brcmfmac/of.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun6i-a31s-sinovoip-bpi-m2.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun7i-a20-bananapi-m1-plus.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun7i-a20-bananapro.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun7i-a20-cubietruck.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun7i-a20-i12-tvbox.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun7i-a20-wits-pro-a20-dkt.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-a33-vstar.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-a83t-bananapi-m3.dts`, and 2 more. The file includes 1 embedded example that should be part of binding validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource-shape changes that still compile but break driver probe, link training, calibration lookup, OPP selection, NVMEM cell reads, or bus enumeration. Tightening required properties can reject existing boards; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, supplies, PHY links, PCIe windows, memory regions, OPP fields, or NVMEM cells. Child-node schemas need special care because address cells, port IDs, PCI interrupt maps, wireless calibration cells, NVMEM layouts, and OPP tables must match driver-side assumptions. This schema closes composed schemas with `unevaluatedProperties: false`. It has 13 top-level properties and 13 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatible variants and DTS users, not just the nominal filename.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/wireless/brcm,bcm4329-fmac.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/wireless/brcm,bcm4329-fmac.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required properties, use invalid compatible fallbacks, corrupt `reg` or interrupt counts, misspell vendor properties, violate child-node address-cell rules, and exercise every conditional branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct subsystem registration, valid NVMEM/OPP/PCIe/wireless/netdev/accelerator/storage behavior as applicable, clean interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no new dtbs_check failures in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/wireless/brcm,bcm4329-fmac.yaml -->

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/wireless/mediatek,mt76.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/wireless/mediatek,mt76.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `MediaTek mt76 wireless devices`, a wireless controller binding. The schema description says: This node provides properties for configuring the MediaTek mt76xx wireless device. The node is expected to be specified as a child node of the PCI controller to which the wireless chip is connected. Alternatively, it can specify the wireless part of the MT7628/MT7688 or MT7622/MT7986 SoC. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input rather than executable kernel code; its practical role is to make DTS/DTB hardware descriptions match the resources, topology, and compatible strings expected by the relevant kernel subsystem before probe.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/wireless/qcom,ath11k-pci.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/wireless/qcom,ath11k-pci.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Qualcomm Technologies ath11k wireless devices (PCIe)`, a wireless controller binding. The schema description says: Qualcomm Technologies IEEE 802.11ax PCIe devices It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input rather than executable kernel code; its practical role is to make DTS/DTB hardware descriptions match the resources, topology, and compatible strings expected by the relevant kernel subsystem before probe.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The public API is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses an `enum` and exposes 2 tokens: `pci17cb,1101`, `pci17cb,1103`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `qcom,calibration-variant`, `qcom,ath11k-calibration-variant`, `firmware-name`, `vddrfacmn-supply`, `vddaon-supply`, `vddwlcx-supply`, `vddwlmx-supply`, `vddrfa0p8-supply`, `vddrfa1p2-supply`, `vddrfa1p7-supply`, `vddrfa1p8-supply`, `vddpcie0p9-supply`, `vddpcie1p8-supply`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`; required properties collected from nested schema branches include `compatible`, `reg`, `vddaon-supply`, `vddpcie0p9-supply`, `vddpcie1p8-supply`, `vddrfa0p8-supply`, `vddrfa1p2-supply`, `vddrfa1p7-supply`, `vddrfa1p8-supply`, `vddrfacmn-supply`, `vddwlcx-supply`, `vddwlmx-supply`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` describes MMIO ranges, bus child addresses, port selectors, or cell offsets; `vddrfacmn-supply` links power rails through regulator providers; `vddaon-supply` links power rails through regulator providers; `vddwlcx-supply` links power rails through regulator providers; `vddwlmx-supply` links power rails through regulator providers; `vddrfa0p8-supply` links power rails through regulator providers; `vddrfa1p2-supply` links power rails through regulator providers; `vddrfa1p7-supply` links power rails through regulator providers; `vddrfa1p8-supply` links power rails through regulator providers; `vddpcie0p9-supply` links power rails through regulator providers; `vddpcie1p8-supply` links power rails through regulator providers. The highest-risk ABI details are compatible identity, bus address, interrupt or wake wiring, calibration and OTP/NVMEM references, firmware names, frequency limits, power limits, and vendor board-data properties.

## Control Flow
Runtime control flow is supplied by dt-schema and kernel subsystem code, not by this YAML file. `dt_binding_check` loads the meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields, validates `minItems`/`maxItems` and enum/const constraints, validates embedded examples, and applies the closure rule. During boot, the compiled DTB node is matched by `compatible`; then platform, PCI, SDIO, SPI, MDIO, NVMEM, OPP, PCIe, netdev, wireless, accelerator, storage, clock, reset, regulator, IRQ, GPIO, IOMMU, and power-domain helpers consume the phandles and resource arrays that this schema validates. Child nodes are discovered declaratively through fixed names, pattern properties, address cells, and referenced child schemas.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The schema owns no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, register and interrupt ordering, phandle and cell counts, child-node names, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in board DTS files and shipped DTBs. Runtime state belongs to the matching drivers and hardware after probe, including wireless device probe, firmware boot, regulatory/channel capability selection, calibration selection, IRQ/wake state, netdev/wiphy registration, and runtime PM.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Jeff Johnson <jjohnson@kernel.org>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/string`. Integration points include cfg80211/mac80211, fullmac or softmac Wi-Fi drivers, SDIO/PCIe/SPI/platform buses, firmware and calibration loading, regulators, clocks, GPIOs, IRQs, NVMEM cells, and board DTS nodes. Driver integration is through OF/ACPI-adjacent match tables and subsystem helper calls; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings, resource arrays, child nodes, and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/pci/pwrctrl/pci-pwrctrl-pwrseq.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/lemans-ride-common.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/qcs615-ride.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/qcs8300-ride.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/qrb5165-rb5.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/sc8280xp-crd.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/sc8280xp-huawei-gaokun3.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/sc8280xp-lenovo-thinkpad-x13s.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/sc8280xp-microsoft-arcata.dts`, and 2 more. The file includes 1 embedded example that should be part of binding validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource-shape changes that still compile but break driver probe, link training, calibration lookup, OPP selection, NVMEM cell reads, or bus enumeration. Tightening required properties can reject existing boards; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, supplies, PHY links, PCIe windows, memory regions, OPP fields, or NVMEM cells. Child-node schemas need special care because address cells, port IDs, PCI interrupt maps, wireless calibration cells, NVMEM layouts, and OPP tables must match driver-side assumptions. This schema rejects unknown top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. It has 15 top-level properties and 15 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatible variants and DTS users, not just the nominal filename.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/wireless/qcom,ath11k-pci.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/wireless/qcom,ath11k-pci.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required properties, use invalid compatible fallbacks, corrupt `reg` or interrupt counts, misspell vendor properties, violate child-node address-cell rules, and exercise every conditional branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct subsystem registration, valid NVMEM/OPP/PCIe/wireless/netdev/accelerator/storage behavior as applicable, clean interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no new dtbs_check failures in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/wireless/qcom,ath11k-pci.yaml -->

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/wireless/qcom,ath12k-wsi.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/wireless/qcom,ath12k-wsi.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Qualcomm Technologies ath12k wireless devices (PCIe) with WSI interface`, a wireless controller binding. The schema description says: Qualcomm Technologies IEEE 802.11be PCIe devices with WSI interface. The ath12k devices (QCN9274) feature WSI support. WSI stands for WLAN Serial Interface. It is used for the exchange of specific control information across radios based on the doorbell mechanism. This WSI connection is essential to exchange control information among these devices. The WSI interface includes TX and RX ports, which are used to connect multiple WSI-supported devices together, forming a WSI group. Diagram to represent one WSI connection (one WSI group) among three devices. +-------+ +-------+ +-------+ | pcie1 | | pcie2 | | pcie3 | | | | | | | +----->| wsi |------->| wsi |------->| wsi |-----+ | | grp 0 | | g... It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input rather than executable kernel code; its practical role is to make DTS/DTB hardware descriptions match the resources, topology, and compatible strings expected by the relevant kernel subsystem before probe.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The public API is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses an `enum` and exposes 1 token: `pci17cb,1109`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `qcom,calibration-variant`, `qcom,ath12k-calibration-variant`, `qcom,wsi-controller`, `ports`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`; required properties collected from nested schema branches include `compatible`, `reg`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` describes MMIO ranges, bus child addresses, port selectors, or cell offsets. The highest-risk ABI details are compatible identity, bus address, interrupt or wake wiring, calibration and OTP/NVMEM references, firmware names, frequency limits, power limits, and vendor board-data properties.

## Control Flow
Runtime control flow is supplied by dt-schema and kernel subsystem code, not by this YAML file. `dt_binding_check` loads the meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates no top-level conditionals, checks required fields, validates `minItems`/`maxItems` and enum/const constraints, validates embedded examples, and applies the closure rule. Child nodes are discovered declaratively through fixed names, pattern properties, address cells, and referenced child schemas.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The schema owns no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, register and interrupt ordering, phandle and cell counts, child-node names, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in board DTS files and shipped DTBs. ## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/wireless/silabs,wfx.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Silicon Labs WFxxx`, a wireless controller binding. The schema description says: Support for the Wifi chip WFxxx from Silicon Labs. Currently, the only device from the WFxxx series is the WF200 described here: https://www.silabs.com/documents/public/data-sheets/wf200-datasheet.pdf The WF200 can be connected via SPI or via SDIO. For SDIO: Declaring the WFxxx chip in device tree is mandatory (usually, the VID/PID is sufficient for the SDIO devices). It is recommended to declare a mmc-pwrseq on SDIO host above WFx. Without it, you may encounter issues during reboot. The mmc-pwrseq should be compatible with mmc-pwrseq-simple. Please consult Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mmc/mmc-pwrseq-simple.yaml for more information. It has 8 top-level properties and 8 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatible variants and DTS users, not just the nominal filename.

Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct subsystem registration, valid NVMEM/OPP/PCIe/wireless/netdev/accelerator/storage behavior as applicable, clean interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no new dtbs_check failures in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/xlnx,axi-ethernet.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/xlnx,axi-ethernet.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `AXI 1G/2.5G Ethernet Subsystem`, a Xilinx Ethernet datapath binding. The schema description says: Also called AXI 1G/2.5G Ethernet Subsystem, the xilinx axi ethernet IP core provides connectivity to an external ethernet PHY supporting different interfaces: MII, GMII, RGMII, SGMII, 1000BaseX. It also includes two segments of memory for buffering TX and RX, as well as the capability of offloading TX/RX checksum calculation off the processor. Management configuration is done through the AXI interface, while payload is sent and received through means of an AXI DMA controller. This driver includes the DMA driver code, so this driver is incompatible with AXI DMA driver. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input rather than executable kernel code; its practical role is to make DTS/DTB hardware descriptions match the resources, topology, and compatible strings expected by the relevant kernel subsystem before probe.

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/xlnx,gmii-to-rgmii.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Xilinx GMII to RGMII Converter`, a Xilinx Ethernet datapath binding. The schema description says: The Gigabit Media Independent Interface (GMII) to Reduced Gigabit Media Independent Interface (RGMII) core provides the RGMII between RGMII-compliant ethernet physical media devices (PHY) and the Gigabit Ethernet controller. This core can be used in all three modes of operation(10/100/1000 Mb/s). The Management Data Input/Output (MDIO) interface is used to configure the speed of operation. This core can switch dynamically between the three different speed modes by configuring the converter register through mdio write. The core cannot function without an external phy connected to it. `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/npu/arm,ethos.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Arm Ethos U65/U85`, a neural processing unit binding. The schema description says: The Arm Ethos-U NPUs are designed for IoT inference applications. The NPUs can accelerate 8-bit and 16-bit integer quantized networks: Transformer networks (U85 only) Convolutional Neural Networks (CNN) Recurrent Neural Networks (RNN) Further documentation is available here: U65 TRM: https://developer.arm.com/documentation/102023/ U85 TRM: https://developer.arm.com/documentation/102685/ It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input rather than executable kernel code; its practical role is to make DTS/DTB hardware descriptions match the resources, topology, and compatible strings expected by the relevant kernel subsystem before probe.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The public API is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses `oneOf` with 2 branchs and exposes 4 tokens: `fsl,imx93-npu`, `arm,ethos-u65`, `arm,corstone1000-ethos-u85`, `arm,ethos-u85`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `power-domains`, `sram`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `clocks`; required properties collected from nested schema branches include `clocks`, `compatible`, `interrupts`, `reg`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`, `maxItems=2`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` describes MMIO ranges, bus child addresses, port selectors, or cell offsets; `interrupts` routes status, error, RX/TX, MSI, or completion IRQs; `clocks` integrates with common clock, reset, PM, DMA/IOMMU, or reserved-memory providers; `clock-names` fixes resource ordering visible to drivers; `power-domains` integrates with common clock, reset, PM, DMA/IOMMU, or reserved-memory providers. The highest-risk ABI details are compute-core compatible identity, MMIO region layout, IRQ ordering, power/clock/reset dependencies, IOMMU stream IDs, SRAM or memory-region phandles, and per-core child topology.

## Control Flow
Runtime control flow is supplied by dt-schema and kernel subsystem code, not by this YAML file. `dt_binding_check` loads the meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates no top-level conditionals, checks required fields, validates `minItems`/`maxItems` and enum/const constraints, validates embedded examples, and applies the closure rule. During boot, the compiled DTB node is matched by `compatible`; then platform, PCI, SDIO, SPI, MDIO, NVMEM, OPP, PCIe, netdev, wireless, accelerator, storage, clock, reset, regulator, IRQ, GPIO, IOMMU, and power-domain helpers consume the phandles and resource arrays that this schema validates. Child nodes are discovered declaratively through fixed names, pattern properties, address cells, and referenced child schemas.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The schema owns no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, register and interrupt ordering, phandle and cell counts, child-node names, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in board DTS files and shipped DTBs. Runtime state belongs to the matching drivers and hardware after probe, including accelerator probe, job queue state, MMU/IOMMU mappings, firmware or microcode state, power-gated core state, and interrupt-driven command completion.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Rob Herring <robh@kernel.org>. Direct schema dependencies are dt-schema core/meta schemas only. Integration points include NPU accelerator platform drivers, DRM/accelerator or misc-device users, firmware/runtime libraries, IOMMU mappings, power domains, clocks, resets, interrupts, and reserved memory. Driver integration is through OF/ACPI-adjacent match tables and subsystem helper calls; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings, resource arrays, child nodes, and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/accel/ethosu/ethosu_drv.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/arm/corstone1000-a320.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/freescale/imx93.dtsi`. The file includes 1 embedded example that should be part of binding validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource-shape changes that still compile but break driver probe, link training, calibration lookup, OPP selection, NVMEM cell reads, or bus enumeration. Tightening required properties can reject existing boards; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, supplies, PHY links, PCIe windows, memory regions, OPP fields, or NVMEM cells. Child-node schemas need special care because address cells, port IDs, PCI interrupt maps, wireless calibration cells, NVMEM layouts, and OPP tables must match driver-side assumptions. This schema rejects unknown top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. It has 7 top-level properties and 7 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatible variants and DTS users, not just the nominal filename.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/npu/arm,ethos.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/npu/arm,ethos.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required properties, use invalid compatible fallbacks, corrupt `reg` or interrupt counts, misspell vendor properties, violate child-node address-cell rules, and exercise every conditional branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct subsystem registration, valid NVMEM/OPP/PCIe/wireless/netdev/accelerator/storage behavior as applicable, clean interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no new dtbs_check failures in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The schema owns no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Maintainers listed: Frank Li <Frank.Li@nxp.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `nvmem-deprecated-cells.yaml`, `nvmem.yaml#`. Integration points include the Linux NVMEM core, SoC eFuse/OTP/EEPROM providers, consumer phandles, clocks, resets, regulators, syscon/regmap access, child cell nodes, and board identity/calibration users. Driver integration is through OF/ACPI-adjacent match tables and subsystem helper calls; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings, resource arrays, child nodes, and phandle topology. ## State and Persistence Behavior
The schema owns no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, register and interrupt ordering, phandle and cell counts, child-node names, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in board DTS files and shipped DTBs. Runtime state belongs to the matching drivers and hardware after probe, including parsed cell inventory, decoded manufacturing data, MAC/serial/board identity lookup, and provider-backed read caching as implemented by the NVMEM core.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Miquel Raynal <miquel.raynal@bootlin.com>. Direct schema dependencies are dt-schema core/meta schemas only. Integration points include the NVMEM layout parser framework, provider child nodes, fixed cells, ONIE TLV or U-Boot environment parsers, MAC-address/serial/EEPROM consumers, and board manufacturing-data descriptions. Driver integration is through OF/ACPI-adjacent match tables and subsystem helper calls; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings, resource arrays, child nodes, and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/nvmem/layouts/onie-tlv.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/riscv/boot/dts/spacemit/k1-bananapi-f3.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/riscv/boot/dts/spacemit/k1-milkv-jupiter.dts`. The file includes 2 embedded examples that should be part of binding validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource-shape changes that still compile but break driver probe, link training, calibration lookup, OPP selection, NVMEM cell reads, or bus enumeration. Tightening required properties can reject existing boards; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, supplies, PHY links, PCIe windows, memory regions, OPP fields, or NVMEM cells. Child-node schemas need special care because address cells, port IDs, PCI interrupt maps, wireless calibration cells, NVMEM layouts, and OPP tables must match driver-side assumptions. This schema rejects unknown top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. It has 17 top-level properties and 18 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatible variants and DTS users, not just the nominal filename.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/nvmem/layouts/onie,tlv-layout.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/nvmem/layouts/onie,tlv-layout.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required properties, use invalid compatible fallbacks, corrupt `reg` or interrupt counts, misspell vendor properties, violate child-node address-cell rules, and exercise every conditional branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct subsystem registration, valid NVMEM/OPP/PCIe/wireless/netdev/accelerator/storage behavior as applicable, clean interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no new dtbs_check failures in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.

`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/nvmem/layouts/u-boot,env.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `U-Boot environment variables layout`, a NVMEM layout binding. Data is stored using U-Boot specific formats (variant specific header and NUL separated key-value pairs). Environment data can be stored on various storage entities, e.g.: 1. Raw flash partition 2. UBI volume This binding allows marking storage device (as containing env data) and specifying used format. Variables can be defined as NVMEM device subnodes. Child nodes are discovered declaratively through fixed names, pattern properties, address cells, and referenced child schemas.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The schema owns no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. ## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Claudiu Beznea <claudiu.beznea@microchip.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `nvmem-deprecated-cells.yaml#`, `nvmem.yaml#`. Integration points include the Linux NVMEM core, SoC eFuse/OTP/EEPROM providers, consumer phandles, clocks, resets, regulators, syscon/regmap access, child cell nodes, and board identity/calibration users. Driver integration is through OF/ACPI-adjacent match tables and subsystem helper calls; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings, resource arrays, child nodes, and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/ata/ahci_imx.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/ata/ahci_mtk.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/ata/pata_ixp4xx_cf.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/ata/sata_gemini.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/auxdisplay/img-ascii-lcd.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/bluetooth/ath3k.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/bluetooth/btusb.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/bus/arm-integrator-lm.c`, and 2 more. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-a83t.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-h3.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-r40.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-v3s.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sunxi-h3-h5.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/amazon/alpine.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/amlogic/meson.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/amlogic/meson8.dtsi`, and 2 more. ## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource-shape changes that still compile but break driver probe, link training, calibration lookup, OPP selection, NVMEM cell reads, or bus enumeration. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include no explicit numeric or item limits collected. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` describes MMIO ranges, bus child addresses, port selectors, or cell offsets. The highest-risk ABI details are provider compatible identity, register ranges, cell address/size units, read-only/writeability policy, clock/reset/power resources, and named cells consumed by drivers.

## Control Flow
Runtime control flow is supplied by dt-schema and kernel subsystem code, not by this YAML file. `dt_binding_check` loads the meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields, validates `minItems`/`maxItems` and enum/const constraints, validates embedded examples, and applies the closure rule. During boot, the compiled DTB node is matched by `compatible`; then platform, PCI, SDIO, SPI, MDIO, NVMEM, OPP, PCIe, netdev, wireless, accelerator, storage, clock, reset, regulator, IRQ, GPIO, IOMMU, and power-domain helpers consume the phandles and resource arrays that this schema validates. Child nodes are discovered declaratively through fixed names, pattern properties, address cells, and referenced child schemas.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The schema owns no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, register and interrupt ordering, phandle and cell counts, child-node names, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in board DTS files and shipped DTBs. Runtime state belongs to the matching drivers and hardware after probe, including NVMEM provider registration, cell lookup tables, OTP/eFuse read sequencing, cached cell values, MAC/calibration/serial-number consumers, and write protection state where supported.

The file includes 1 embedded example that should be part of binding validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource-shape changes that still compile but break driver probe, link training, calibration lookup, OPP selection, NVMEM cell reads, or bus enumeration. Tightening required properties can reject existing boards; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, supplies, PHY links, PCIe windows, memory regions, OPP fields, or NVMEM cells. Child-node schemas need special care because address cells, port IDs, PCI interrupt maps, wireless calibration cells, NVMEM layouts, and OPP tables must match driver-side assumptions. This schema closes composed schemas with `unevaluatedProperties: false`. It has 2 top-level properties and 2 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatible variants and DTS users, not just the nominal filename.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/nvmem/qcom,sec-qfprom.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/nvmem/qcom,sec-qfprom.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required properties, use invalid compatible fallbacks, corrupt `reg` or interrupt counts, misspell vendor properties, violate child-node address-cell rules, and exercise every conditional branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct subsystem registration, valid NVMEM/OPP/PCIe/wireless/netdev/accelerator/storage behavior as applicable, clean interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no new dtbs_check failures in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The public API is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses an `enum` and exposes 7 tokens: `rockchip,px30-otp`, `rockchip,rk3308-otp`, `rockchip,rk3528-otp`, `rockchip,rk3562-otp`, `rockchip,rk3568-otp`, `rockchip,rk3576-otp`, `rockchip,rk3588-otp`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `resets`, `reset-names`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `resets`, `reset-names`; required properties collected from nested schema branches include `clock-names`, `clocks`, `compatible`, `reg`, `reset-names`, `resets`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`, `minItems=3`, `maxItems=4`, `minItems=1`, `maxItems=3`, `minItems=4`, `minItems=2`, `maxItems=2`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` describes MMIO ranges, bus child addresses, port selectors, or cell offsets; `clocks` integrates with common clock, reset, PM, DMA/IOMMU, or reserved-memory providers; `clock-names` fixes resource ordering visible to drivers; `resets` integrates with common clock, reset, PM, DMA/IOMMU, or reserved-memory providers; `reset-names` fixes resource ordering visible to drivers. The highest-risk ABI details are provider compatible identity, register ranges, cell address/size units, read-only/writeability policy, clock/reset/power resources, and named cells consumed by drivers.

## Control Flow
Runtime control flow is supplied by dt-schema and kernel subsystem code, not by this YAML file. `dt_binding_check` loads the meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields, validates `minItems`/`maxItems` and enum/const constraints, validates embedded examples, and applies the closure rule. During boot, the compiled DTB node is matched by `compatible`; then platform, PCI, SDIO, SPI, MDIO, NVMEM, OPP, PCIe, netdev, wireless, accelerator, storage, clock, reset, regulator, IRQ, GPIO, IOMMU, and power-domain helpers consume the phandles and resource arrays that this schema validates. Child nodes are discovered declaratively through fixed names, pattern properties, address cells, and referenced child schemas.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The schema owns no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, register and interrupt ordering, phandle and cell counts, child-node names, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in board DTS files and shipped DTBs. Runtime state belongs to the matching drivers and hardware after probe, including NVMEM provider registration, cell lookup tables, OTP/eFuse read sequencing, cached cell values, MAC/calibration/serial-number consumers, and write protection state where supported.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Heiko Stuebner <heiko@sntech.de>. Direct schema dependencies are `nvmem-deprecated-cells.yaml#`, `nvmem.yaml#`. Integration points include the Linux NVMEM core, SoC eFuse/OTP/EEPROM providers, consumer phandles, clocks, resets, regulators, syscon/regmap access, child cell nodes, and board identity/calibration users. Driver integration is through OF/ACPI-adjacent match tables and subsystem helper calls; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings, resource arrays, child nodes, and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/nvmem/rockchip-otp.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/rockchip/px30.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/rockchip/rk3308.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/rockchip/rk3528.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/rockchip/rk3562.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/rockchip/rk356x-base.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/rockchip/rk3576.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/rockchip/rk3588-base.dtsi`. The file includes 1 embedded example that should be part of binding validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource-shape changes that still compile but break driver probe, link training, calibration lookup, OPP selection, NVMEM cell reads, or bus enumeration. Tightening required properties can reject existing boards; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, supplies, PHY links, PCIe windows, memory regions, OPP fields, or NVMEM cells. Child-node schemas need special care because address cells, port IDs, PCI interrupt maps, wireless calibration cells, NVMEM layouts, and OPP tables must match driver-side assumptions. This schema closes composed schemas with `unevaluatedProperties: false`. It has 6 top-level properties and 6 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatible variants and DTS users, not just the nominal filename.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/nvmem/rockchip,otp.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/nvmem/rockchip,otp.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required properties, use invalid compatible fallbacks, corrupt `reg` or interrupt counts, misspell vendor properties, violate child-node address-cell rules, and exercise every conditional branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct subsystem registration, valid NVMEM/OPP/PCIe/wireless/netdev/accelerator/storage behavior as applicable, clean interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no new dtbs_check failures in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/opp/allwinner,sun50i-h6-operating-points.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/opp/allwinner,sun50i-h6-operating-points.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Allwinner H6 CPU OPP`, a operating-points binding. The schema description says: For some SoCs, the CPU frequency subset and voltage value of each OPP varies based on the silicon variant in use. Allwinner Process Voltage Scaling Tables define the voltage and frequency values based on the speedbin blown in the efuse combination. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input rather than executable kernel code; its practical role is to make DTS/DTB hardware descriptions match the resources, topology, and compatible strings expected by the relevant kernel subsystem before probe.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The public API is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses an `enum` and exposes 3 tokens: `allwinner,sun50i-a100-operating-points`, `allwinner,sun50i-h6-operating-points`, `allwinner,sun50i-h616-operating-points`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `nvmem-cells`, `opp-shared`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `nvmem-cells`; required properties collected from nested schema branches include `compatible`, `nvmem-cells`, `opp-hz`. Patterned child schemas are `^opp-[0-9]+$`. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `nvmem-cells` links calibration, MAC, serial, or speed-bin data through the NVMEM core. The highest-risk ABI details are OPP table compatibility, `opp-hz`, voltage/current/microvolt arrays, level/performance-state values, supported-hw masks, turbo/suspend flags, named supplies, and required-opps links.

## Control Flow
Runtime control flow is supplied by dt-schema and kernel subsystem code, not by this YAML file. `dt_binding_check` loads the meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields, validates `minItems`/`maxItems` and enum/const constraints, validates embedded examples, and applies the closure rule. Child nodes are discovered declaratively through fixed names, pattern properties, address cells, and referenced child schemas.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The schema owns no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, register and interrupt ordering, phandle and cell counts, child-node names, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in board DTS files and shipped DTBs. Runtime state belongs to the matching drivers and hardware after probe, including registered OPP tables, enabled/disabled performance points, regulator voltage/current selection, clock-rate transitions, power-domain state votes, cooling state limits, and speed-bin filtering.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/opp/opp-v2-kryo-cpu.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/opp/opp-v2-kryo-cpu.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Qualcomm Technologies, Inc. NVMEM OPP`, a operating-points binding. The schema description says: In certain Qualcomm Technologies, Inc. SoCs like APQ8096 and MSM8996, the CPU frequencies subset and voltage value of each OPP varies based on the silicon variant in use. Qualcomm Technologies, Inc. Process Voltage Scaling Tables defines the voltage and frequency value based on the speedbin blown in the efuse combination. The qcom-cpufreq-nvmem driver reads the efuse value from the SoC to provide the OPP framework with required information (existing HW bitmap). This is used to determine the voltage and frequency value for each OPP of operating-points-v2 table when it is parsed by the OPP framework. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input rather than executable kernel code; its practical role is to make DTS/DTB hardware descriptions match the resources, topology, and compatible strings expected by the relevant kernel subsystem before probe.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The public API is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses an `enum` and exposes 2 tokens: `operating-points-v2-krait-cpu`, `operating-points-v2-kryo-cpu`. The highest-risk ABI details are OPP table compatibility, `opp-hz`, voltage/current/microvolt arrays, level/performance-state values, supported-hw masks, turbo/suspend flags, named supplies, and required-opps links.

## Control Flow

During boot, the compiled DTB node is matched by `compatible`; then platform, PCI, SDIO, SPI, MDIO, NVMEM, OPP, PCIe, netdev, wireless, accelerator, storage, clock, reset, regulator, IRQ, GPIO, IOMMU, and power-domain helpers consume the phandles and resource arrays that this schema validates. ## State and Persistence Behavior
The schema owns no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. ## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/opp/ti,omap-opp-supply.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Texas Instruments OMAP compatible OPP supply`, a operating-points binding. The schema description says: OMAP5, DRA7, and AM57 families of SoCs have Class 0 AVS eFuse registers, which contain OPP-specific voltage information tailored for the specific device. This binding provides the information needed to describe such a hardware values and relate them to program the primary regulator during an OPP transition. Also, some supplies may have an associated vbb-supply, an Adaptive Body Bias regulator, which must transition in a specific sequence w.r.t the vdd-supply and clk when making an OPP transition. By supplying two regulators to the device that will undergo OPP transitions, we can use the multi-regulator support implemented by the OPP core to describe both regulators the platform needs. The... It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input rather than executable kernel code; its practical role is to make DTS/DTB hardware descriptions match the resources, topology, and compatible strings expected by the relevant kernel subsystem before probe.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/pci/altr,pcie-root-port.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/pci/altr,pcie-root-port.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Altera PCIe Root Port`, a PCIe host or endpoint controller binding. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input rather than executable kernel code; its practical role is to make DTS/DTB hardware descriptions match the resources, topology, and compatible strings expected by the relevant kernel subsystem before probe.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The public API is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses an `enum` and exposes 5 tokens: `altr,pcie-root-port-1.0`, `altr,pcie-root-port-2.0`, `altr,pcie-root-port-3.0-f-tile`, `altr,pcie-root-port-3.0-p-tile`, `altr,pcie-root-port-3.0-r-tile`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `reg-names`, `interrupts`, `interrupt-controller`, `interrupt-map-mask`, `interrupt-map`, `#interrupt-cells`, `msi-parent`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `reg-names`, `interrupts`, `#interrupt-cells`, `interrupt-controller`, `interrupt-map`, `interrupt-map-mask`; required properties collected from nested schema branches include `#interrupt-cells`, `compatible`, `interrupt-controller`, `interrupt-map`, `interrupt-map-mask`, `interrupts`, `reg`, `reg-names`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `minItems=2`, `maxItems=1`, `maxItems=4`, `maxItems=2`, `minItems=3`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` describes MMIO ranges, bus child addresses, port selectors, or cell offsets; `reg-names` fixes resource ordering visible to drivers; `interrupts` routes status, error, RX/TX, MSI, or completion IRQs; `interrupt-map` models PCI INTx swizzling; `#interrupt-cells` defines child-node or phandle specifier shape. The highest-risk ABI details are compatible fallback ordering, `reg`/`reg-names`, `ranges`, bus-range, MSI and interrupt-map wiring, PHY/reset/clock ordering, PERST#/wake GPIOs, link-generation and lane-count properties, and endpoint vs root-complex mode constraints.

## Control Flow
Runtime control flow is supplied by dt-schema and kernel subsystem code, not by this YAML file. `dt_binding_check` loads the meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields, validates `minItems`/`maxItems` and enum/const constraints, validates embedded examples, and applies the closure rule. During boot, the compiled DTB node is matched by `compatible`; then platform, PCI, SDIO, SPI, MDIO, NVMEM, OPP, PCIe, netdev, wireless, accelerator, storage, clock, reset, regulator, IRQ, GPIO, IOMMU, and power-domain helpers consume the phandles and resource arrays that this schema validates. Child nodes are discovered declaratively through fixed names, pattern properties, address cells, and referenced child schemas.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The schema owns no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, register and interrupt ordering, phandle and cell counts, child-node names, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in board DTS files and shipped DTBs. Runtime state belongs to the matching drivers and hardware after probe, including PCI host bridge registration, ECAM/config-space access, link training state, MSI domain state, DMA/IOMMU windows, endpoint BAR/function configuration, reset recovery, and runtime PM.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Matthew Gerlach <matthew.gerlach@linux.intel.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/pci/pci-host-bridge.yaml#`. Integration points include Linux PCI host bridge or endpoint controller drivers, DesignWare/Cadence/vendor PCIe glue, MSI/INTx interrupt domains, PHY/SerDes lanes, resets, clocks, regulators, IOMMU/DMA windows, address translation ranges, and board DTS root-complex or endpoint nodes. Driver integration is through OF/ACPI-adjacent match tables and subsystem helper calls; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings, resource arrays, child nodes, and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/pci/controller/pcie-altera.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: no in-tree DTS users found by compatible scan. The file includes 1 embedded example that should be part of binding validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource-shape changes that still compile but break driver probe, link training, calibration lookup, OPP selection, NVMEM cell reads, or bus enumeration. Tightening required properties can reject existing boards; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, supplies, PHY links, PCIe windows, memory regions, OPP fields, or NVMEM cells. Child-node schemas need special care because address cells, port IDs, PCI interrupt maps, wireless calibration cells, NVMEM layouts, and OPP tables must match driver-side assumptions. This schema closes composed schemas with `unevaluatedProperties: false`. It has 9 top-level properties and 9 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatible variants and DTS users, not just the nominal filename.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/pci/altr,pcie-root-port.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/pci/altr,pcie-root-port.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required properties, use invalid compatible fallbacks, corrupt `reg` or interrupt counts, misspell vendor properties, violate child-node address-cell rules, and exercise every conditional branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct subsystem registration, valid NVMEM/OPP/PCIe/wireless/netdev/accelerator/storage behavior as applicable, clean interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no new dtbs_check failures in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/pci/apple,pcie.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/pci/apple,pcie.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Apple PCIe host controller`, a PCIe host or endpoint controller binding. The schema description says: The Apple PCIe host controller is a PCIe host controller with multiple root ports present in Apple ARM SoC platforms, including various iPhone and iPad devices and the "Apple Silicon" Macs. The controller incorporates Synopsys DesigWare PCIe logic to implements its root ports. But the ATU found on most DesignWare PCIe host bridges is absent. On systems derived from T602x, the PHY registers are in a region separate from the port registers. In that case, there is one PHY register range per port register range. All root ports share a single ECAM space, but separate GPIOs are used to take the PCI devices on those ports out of reset. Therefore the standard "reset-gpios" and "max-link-speed" pr... Driver integration is through OF/ACPI-adjacent match tables and subsystem helper calls; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings, resource arrays, child nodes, and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/pci/controller/dwc/pcie-artpec6.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/pci/controller/dwc/pcie-designware-plat.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/pci/controller/pci-host-generic.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/axis/artpec6.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/st/spear1310.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/st/spear1340.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/ti/keystone/keystone-k2e.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/ti/keystone/keystone.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/amlogic/meson-axg.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/amlogic/meson-g12-common.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/marvell/armada-cp11x.dtsi`. The file includes 1 embedded example that should be part of binding validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource-shape changes that still compile but break driver probe, link training, calibration lookup, OPP selection, NVMEM cell reads, or bus enumeration. Tightening required properties can reject existing boards; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, supplies, PHY links, PCIe windows, memory regions, OPP fields, or NVMEM cells. Child-node schemas need special care because address cells, port IDs, PCI interrupt maps, wireless calibration cells, NVMEM layouts, and OPP tables must match driver-side assumptions. This schema closes composed schemas with `unevaluatedProperties: false`. It has 6 top-level properties and 6 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatible variants and DTS users, not just the nominal filename.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/pci/axis,artpec6-pcie.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/pci/axis,artpec6-pcie.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required properties, use invalid compatible fallbacks, corrupt `reg` or interrupt counts, misspell vendor properties, violate child-node address-cell rules, and exercise every conditional branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct subsystem registration, valid NVMEM/OPP/PCIe/wireless/netdev/accelerator/storage behavior as applicable, clean interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no new dtbs_check failures in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.
